2913 ABBC A1FA     	data define_char
2914               	; 	' Without this the font keeps whatever CVBasic left in the colour table --
2915               	; 	' white on transparent -- which over a green store made the HUD unreadable.
2916               	; 	'
2917               	; 	' THIS WAS A VPOKE LOOP AND IT WAS THE SLOWEST THING IN THE BOOT: 1,416
2918               	; 	' writes of one constant, paced by 24 WAITs, which is what made the title
2919               	; 	' fill in visibly instead of appearing. DEFINE COLOR does the same job in a
2920               	; 	' single call with interrupts off, and it writes all three screen thirds
2921               	; 	' itself -- define_color always takes the LDIRVM3 triple-copy path, which is
2922               	; 	' also why esc_deck_col and floor0_colour below CANNOT use it: they patch
2923               	; 	' one third each.
2924               	; 	'
2925               	; 	' The table is 472 identical bytes (genfont.py emits it beside the glyphs).
2926               	; 	' It reads as waste and is not: it lives in the data bank, which had 860
2927               	; 	' bytes spare, while the loop it replaced cost time in the one place the
2928               	; 	' player is made to wait.
2929               	; 	DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col
2930 ABBE 0204  20 	li r4,32
     ABC0 0020     
2931 ABC2 0205  20 	li r5,15104
     ABC4 3B00     
2932 ABC6 0200  20 	li r0,cvb_FONT_COL
     ABC8 6202     
2933 ABCA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     ABCC A980     
2934 ABCE A22E     	data define_color
2935               	; 	RETURN

2939               	; 	' EVERYTHING THE TITLE DOES NOT NEED -- and it still runs BEFORE the

2941               	; 	' that is a second late. Kept separate from setup_font because `boot`
2942               	; 	' re-enters below it: a game over redraws the title without rebuilding a
2943               	; 	' store that is already defined.

3223               	;
3224               	; 	' THE JUMP ARC IS A TABLE, not integration. That makes the 14 px apex a
3225               	; 	' property of the data rather than of a fixed-point velocity that has to
3226               	; 	' be tuned, and it keeps every comparison in the jump 8-bit and unsigned.
3227               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a depends on the apex being exactly 14.
3228               	; 	'
3229               	; 	' THIRTY FRAMES WITH NINE OF THEM AT THE APEX. The first version was 24
3230               	; 	' frames that touched 14 for only four, which made every jump a timing
3231               	; 	' test rather than a decision -- you had to leave the ground on exactly
3232               	; 	' the right frame or clip the thing you were jumping. Widening the
3233               	; 	' plateau rather than raising the apex keeps the ball arithmetic in
3234               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a intact (the apex is what that depends on) while making the
3235               	; 	' window forgiving: the player still has to CHOOSE to jump, but no longer
3236               	; 	' has to be frame-perfect about it.
3237               	; 	' THE ARC IS A TABLE, not thirty assignments -- each of those compiled to
3238               	; 	' several bytes of code, and the fixed area is the binding budget.
3239               	; 	#jat = VARPTR jarc_tbl(0)
3240 AE4E 0200  20 	li r0,cvb_JARC_TBL
     AE50 7DD8     
3241 AE52 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__JAT
     AE54 20A4     
3242               	; 	FOR ji = 0 TO 29
3243 AE56 04C0  14 	clr r0
3244 AE58 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_JI
     AE5A 21CE     
3245               cv29
3246               	; 		#jaa = #jat + ji
3247 AE5C D020  34 	movb @cvb_JI,r0
     AE5E 21CE     
3248 AE60 0980  32 	srl r0,8
3249 AE62 C040  18 	mov r0,r1
3250 AE64 C020  34 	mov @cvb__JAT,r0
     AE66 20A4     
3251 AE68 A001  18 	a r1,r0
3252 AE6A C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__JAA
     AE6C 2096     
3253               	; 		jarc(ji) = PEEK(#jaa)
3254 AE6E D050  26 	movb *r0,r1

3291 AECC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3292 AECE 0450  20 	b *r0
3293               	;
3294               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
3295               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
3296               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
3297               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
3298               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
3299               	; 	'
3300               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
3301               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
3302               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
3303               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
3304               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
3305               	; 	'
3306               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
3307               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
3308               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
3309               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. >2000 is the colour table's base
3310               	; 	' and >800 the stride between screen thirds -- the same layout DEFINE COLOR
3311               	; 	' walks for us in setup_font, which this cannot use because it is cheaper to
3312               	; 	' expand 24 bytes here than to ship 384 in the bank.
3313               	; 	' NOTHING IS UNDER THE GROUND FLOOR, SO NOTHING SHOULD BE GREEN THERE.
3314               	; 	' A floor bar is five pixels of yellow over three of the green air
3315               	; 	' belonging to the floor BELOW it (see SLAB), which is right for three of
3316               	; 	' the four bars and wrong for the last one: below floor 0 there is no
3317               	; 	' floor, only the scanner, and the green read as a strip of shop with
3318               	; 	' nothing in it.
3319               	; 	'
3320               	; 	' NO NEW CHARACTER IS NEEDED, BECAUSE THE COLOUR TABLE HAS THREE COPIES
3321               	; 	' -- one per eight screen rows -- and the bands land such that each bar
3322               	; 	' sits in a different third:
3323               	; 	'
3324               	; 	'     band 0 roof   rows  1-5    bar row  5   third 0
3325               	; 	'     band 1        rows  6-10   bar row 10   third 1
3326               	; 	'     band 2        rows 11-15   bar row 15   third 1
3327               	; 	'     band 3 GROUND rows 16-20   bar row 20   third 2
3328               	; 	'
3329               	; 	' so recolouring SLAB in third 2 alone reaches the ground floor's bar and
3330               	; 	' nothing else. The same third also holds the scanner, but the scanner
3331               	; 	' does not use these characters. This is the same mechanism that once hid
3332               	; 	' a bug for months (CLAUDE.md 3A, the blanked-in-one-third note) used
3333               	; 	' deliberately for once.
3334               	; 	'
3335               	; 	' Only the three bottom lines change, and only their BACKGROUND: SLAB has
3336               	; 	' no ink there and SLABE's brick keeps its grey.
3337               	; floor0_colour:
3338               cvb_FLOOR0_COLOUR
3339               	; 	' TWO CHARACTERS, THE SAME THREE SCAN LINES, ONE ROUTINE. Both blocks
3340               	; 	' were written out in full and differed only in which character and which
3341               	; 	' colour byte -- eleven statements twice, including the multiply-by-eight
3342               	; 	' done by doubling (`*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, which clobbers r0,
3343               	; 	' CLAUDE.md 3A).
3344               	; 	f0c = CH_SLAB
3345 AED0 0200  20 	li r0,24576   ; 96*256
     AED2 6000     
3346 AED4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AED6 20CE     
3347               	; 	f0v = 177			' LYELL on BLACK -- no ink, so the
3348 AED8 0200  20 	li r0,45312   ; 177*256
     AEDA B100     
3349 AEDC D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AEDE 20D6     
3350               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows			' background is all that shows
3351 AEE0 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AEE2 A980     
3352 AEE4 AF00     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3353               	; 	f0c = CH_SLABE
3354 AEE6 0200  20 	li r0,24832   ; 97*256
     AEE8 6100     
3355 AEEA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0C
     AEEC 20CE     
3356               	; 	f0v = 225			' GRAY brick on BLACK
3357 AEEE 0200  20 	li r0,57600   ; 225*256
     AEF0 E100     
3358 AEF2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_F0V
     AEF4 20D6     
3359               	; 	GOSUB f0_rows
3360 AEF6 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     AEF8 A980     
3361 AEFA AF00     	data cvb_F0_ROWS
3362               	; 	RETURN

3704               	; 	' The NOTICE is redrawn on every title visit even though the MEASUREMENT
3705               	; 	' happens once -- it is information about the machine, and the CLS above
3706               	; 	' just wiped it.
3707               	; 	RETURN
3708 B280 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
3709 B282 0450  20 	b *r0
3710               	;
3711               	; 	' NO ALPHA LOCK CALIBRATION, AND THERE USED TO BE ONE.
3712               	; 	'
3713               	; 	' The premise was that ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's
3714               	; 	' VERTICAL axis, so a latched key reports a direction that is never
3715               	; 	' released -- fatal here, where down is the duck and up is the lift. The
3716               	; 	' guard sampled the axis for forty frames before listening and then
3717               	; 	' ignored any direction that had been held for essentially all of them.
3718               	; 	'
3719               	; 	' IT WAS REMOVED BECAUSE THE PREMISE DOES NOT HOLD UP.
3720               	; 	'
3721               	; 	'   * CLAUDE.md claimed every other game here dodged the problem by not
3722               	; 	'     reading up/down. EIGHT of them do read it -- Adventire, Astiroids,
3723               	; 	'     HardHatMack, Ms. Pac-Man, RallyX, Structris, UFO, Bust-A-Bobble --
3724               	; 	'     and not one has a calibration or has ever shown the fault.
3725               	; 	'   * It never fired on the machine this is developed on: the notice it
3726               	; 	'     prints when it detects a stuck axis has never appeared.
3727               	; 	'   * The original evidence was that the game "came up and would not
3728               	; 	'     start" -- and the first version BLOCKED until the axis cleared. That
3729               	; 	'     symptom is indistinguishable from the several input bugs since found
3730               	; 	'     and fixed for real: keys arriving at a screen that was not listening
3731               	; 	'     yet. The diagnosis was probably one of those.
3732               	; 	'
3733               	; 	' It also cost two bugs of its own: forty frames of a drawn but deaf title
3734               	; 	' screen, which swallowed the first digit of 8-3-8, and a wrong theory
3735               	; 	' about keyboard noise that led to a filter which ate real presses.
3736               	; 	'
3737               	; 	' If a stuck axis ever does appear it will be obvious -- the player will
3738               	; 	' duck or ride the lift without asking -- and the guard is in the history.
3739               	;
3740               	; title_input:
3741               cvb_TITLE_INPUT
3742               	; 	' THE PROMPT IS PRINTED HERE, NOT WITH THE REST OF THE TITLE, because
3743               	; 	' this is the moment the game starts listening -- and it is a whole
3744               	; 	' second after the title appears.
3745               	; 	'
3746               	; 	' title_draw runs early on purpose (DESIGN.md 0d-nonies) so the screen
3747               	; 	' arrives instead of filling in, but setup_rest, init_tables and the
3748               	; 	' ALPHA LOCK sample all run AFTER it. For about a second the title is up,
3749               	; 	' finished, and completely deaf. A player types 8-3-8 into that window and
3750               	; 	' the first digit lands in nothing -- then a following 3-8 completes the
3751               	; 	' code, because the 8 they typed second is still standing as state. That
3752               	; 	' is exactly how it was reported, twice, and it is not noise: it is the
3753               	; 	' screen lying about being ready.
3754               	; 	'
3755               	; 	' RallyX and Bust-A-Bobble never showed this because they draw their
3756               	; 	' titles when they are already listening. Keystone cannot -- the whole
3757               	; 	' point of the early draw is that it is early -- so the PROMPT waits
3758               	; 	' instead, and its arrival is the cue that the screen is awake.
3759               	; 	PRINT AT 614,"FIRE TO START"

3889               	;
3890               	; 	' 8-3-8 IS EDGE-TRIGGERED AND ANY STRAY DIGIT RESETS IT (see title_wait).
3891               	; 	'
3892               	; 	' It used to read cont1.key raw every pass and test only for the digit it
3893               	; 	' wanted next. That worked -- but only because 8-3-8 ALTERNATES, so holding
3894               	; 	' 8 cannot advance past the first step. It is an accident of the sequence
3895               	; 	' rather than a design, and it had two costs: 8,5,3,9,8 opened this page as
3896               	; 	' readily as 8,3,8, and a key that READS as held for many frames got a free
3897               	; 	' walk through the state machine.
3898               	; 	'
3899               	; 	' That second one is not hypothetical on this machine. ALPHA LOCK shorts a
3900               	; 	' keyboard line -- and Classic99 defaults to invertcaps, so it reads DOWN
3901               	; 	' with the host's Caps Lock UP -- and this page was reached twice from
3902               	; 	' single keypresses while testing an unrelated change.
3903               	; 	'
3904               	; 	' The reset is the DEFAULT rather than a test for a particular wrong digit:
3905               	; 	' Bust-A-Bobble resets only on a stray 3 and still lets 8,5,3,8 through.
3906               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------- 838 setup page
3907               	; setup838:
3908               cvb_SETUP838

3912               	; 	' ONE PROMPT AT A TIME, NOTHING ELSE ON THE SCREEN, AND NO NUMBERS UNTIL
3913               	; 	' THEY ARE TYPED. There is no heading and no instructions: a page showing
3914               	; 	' exactly one question does not need to explain that one digit answers it,
3915               	; 	' and the second question does not exist until the first is answered.
3916               	; 	'
3917               	; 	' Showing the CURRENT values first was the obvious thing and it cost more
3918               	; 	' than it gave -- two draw routines, a "fire keeps what is shown" escape to
3919               	; 	' make the display mean something, and the defaults had to be applied here
3920               	; 	' as well as in new_game so the page had numbers to show at all. None of it
3921               	; 	' survives: every digit on screen is one the player just typed. Four
3922               	; 	' strings became two, a two-field loop became a straight line, and the page
3923               	; 	' got SMALLER while getting quieter.

4301               	; 	' never reset when play began, so it carried its value in from whatever
4302               	; 	' happened last. Press FIRE on the title with jrel left at 1 and Kelly

4342               	; 	' values straddled the buzzer with nothing in between:
4343               	; 	'
4344               	; 	'   2.00 px/pass   escapes at 102.4 s of a 100 s round   never escapes
4345               	; 	'   2.25 px/pass   escapes at  91.0 s                    nine seconds early
4346               	; 	'

4350               	; 	' own escape. See the accumulator comment for the full account.
4351               	; 	'
4352               	; 	' Sixteenths with three drains put the step at about 2.5 s instead of 10,

4368               	; 	' The margin

4589 B802 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4590 B804 0450  20 	b *r0
4591               	;
4592               	; 	' ======================================================================
4593               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4594               	; 	' ======================================================================
4595               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4596               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4597               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4598               	; 	' of running.
4599               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4600               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4601               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4602               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4603               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4604               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4605               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4606               	; 	'
4607               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4608               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4609               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4610               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4611               	; 	'
4612               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4613               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4614               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4615               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4616               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4617               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4618               	; 	'
4619               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4620               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4621               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4622               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4623               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4624               	; 	'
4625               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4626               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4627               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4628               	; draw_screen:
4629               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4630               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4631 B806 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B808 A980     
4632 B80A EB88     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4633               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4634 B80C 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B80E A980     
4635 B810 BBFA     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND
4636               	; 	FOR dq = 0 TO 3
4637 B812 04C0  14 	clr r0
4638 B814 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_DQ
     B816 21B3     
4639               cv119
4640               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4641               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4642               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4643               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4644               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4645               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4646               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4647               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4648               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4649               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4738               	;
4739               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4740               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4741               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4742               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4743               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4744               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4745               	; 	'
4746               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4747               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4748               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4749               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4750               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4751               	; 	'
4752               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4753               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4754               	; 	' above it at all.
4755               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4756               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4757               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4758               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4759               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4760               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4761               	; 	'
4762               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4763               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4764               	; beam_one:
4765               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4766               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

5299               	; 			' AND HOW MANY FLOORS CARRY ONE AT ALL. The gates above
5300               	; 			' decide what KIND a hazard is; this decides whether the
5301               	; 			' floor has one.
5302               	; 			'
5303               	; 			' The kind-arrival rule downgrades anything that has not
5304               	; 			' arrived yet to a beach ball rather than leaving the floor
5305               	; 			' empty, so that a floor is never bare -- and the effect on
5306               	; 			' Krook 1 was a ball on all FOUR floors of every populated
5307               	; 			' screen. All four bands are on screen at once, so that is
5308               	; 			' four identical balls in view at all times, which is not
5309               	; 			' what "merely a few beach balls" describes.
5310               	; 			'
5311               	; 			' Per-band count was never the problem: only one slot is
5312               	; 			' live until Krook 6. It is the number of OCCUPIED FLOORS,
5313               	; 			' and it had no ramp at all.
5314               	; 			'
5315               	; 			' Floors 1 and 3 stay clear on Krook 1, floor 1 joins on
5316               	; 			' Krook 2, and the roof on Krook 3. ALTERNATING rather than
5317               	; 			' clearing the top or bottom half, so the empty floors do
5318               	; 			' not stack into a visibly dead region that reads as a bug
5319               	; 			' -- and the ROOF is last, because that is where the round
5320               	; 			' is decided.
5321               	; 			IF krk < 2 THEN

5359               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
5360               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
5361               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
5362               	; 			'
5363               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
5364               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
5365               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
5366               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
5367               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
5368               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
5369               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
5370               	; 			'
5371               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests

5409 BDD2 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5410               	; 			' A RADIO NEEDS A CELL, NOT A PIXEL, because it is drawn as
5411               	; 			' characters. Divided by repeated subtraction: `/` compiles
5412               	; 			' to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A), and this runs once
5413               	; 			' per screen where the divide would run per obstacle -- so
5414               	; 			' the loop is the cheaper of the two, and the only one that
5415               	; 			' cannot be got wrong by a rounding rule.
5416               	; 			obc(li) = 0
5417 BDD4 04C1  14 	clr r1
5418 BDD6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LI,r0
     BDD8 21DC     
5419 BDDA 0980  32 	srl r0,8
5420 BDDC 0220  22 	ai r0,array_OBC
     BDDE 2270     
5421 BDE0 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5422               	; 			' PER KIND, not one global speed (see start_krook).
5423               	; 			' THE SPEED IS A PROPERTY OF THE KIND, and obs() only ever
5424               	; 			' held a copy of it -- set from the kind here, zeroed for a
5425               	; 			' radio below, and read in one place. upd_obst derives it
5426               	; 			' from obk() instead, which is 8 bytes of RAM back and one
5427               	; 			' fewer thing that can disagree with itself.
5428               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
5429               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5430               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5431               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5432               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5433               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.
5434               	; 			'
5435               	; 			' The table's x becomes a stagger from that edge rather than
5436               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads them out
5437               	; 			' so they arrive in sequence instead of as one wall.
5438               	; 			'
5439               	; 			' THE SECOND ONE IS A FIXED DISTANCE BEHIND THE FIRST, AND
5440               	; 			' THAT DISTANCE IS SET BY THE JUMP.
5441               	; 			'
5442               	; 			' It used to be `(lx AND 63) + 64` -- a masked byte out of
5443               	; 			' the placement table plus a constant -- so the gap between
5444               	; 			' the two hazards was whatever those two bytes happened to
5445               	; 			' differ by. Measured across the five populated screens it
5446               	; 			' came out 46, 46, 70, 70, 70 px. Screens 2 and 4 flank the
5447               	; 			' elevator, so the store had a visibly tighter pair on one
5448               	; 			' side of it than the other, which is exactly how it was
5449               	; 			' reported from play.
5450               	; 			'
5451               	; 			' Both numbers are too small, which is the real fault. Kelly
5452               	; 			' closes on an oncoming hazard at WALKSP + the hazard's own
5453               	; 			' speed = 6 px a pass, and a jump lasts about 30 passes, so
5454               	; 			' a jump eats ~120 px of closing distance. At 46 or 70 there
5455               	; 			' is NO screen where he can land between the two: he clears
5456               	; 			' the first and comes down on the second.
5457               	; 			'
5458               	; 			' HAZGAP is that distance with margin. The first hazard
5459               	; 			' still lands where the table puts it, so the placement
5460               	; 			' variety is unchanged; only the pairing is now a fact about
5461               	; 			' the jump rather than an accident of two bytes.
5462               	; 			' assets/checkspace.py measures it and fails the build.
5463               	; 			' THE SECOND SLOT IS MEASURED FROM THE FIRST, not from its
5464               	; 			' own table byte. Adding HAZGAP to its own `lx AND 63` would
5465               	; 			' still leave the GAP varying by up to 63 px between screens
5466               	; 			' -- the same fault in a politer form, and the reason the two
5467               	; 			' sides of the elevator differed in the first place. Holding
5468               	; 			' slot 0's stagger and adding to that makes the gap exactly
5469               	; 			' HAZGAP on every screen, while slot 0 still lands wherever
5470               	; 			' the table puts it, so the placement variety is untouched.
5471               	; 			stag = lx AND 63
5472 BDE2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0

5552               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5553               	; 			'
5554               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5555               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5556               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5557               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5558               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5559               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5560               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5561               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5562               	; 			'
5563               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5564               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5565               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5566               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5567               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5568               	; 			'
5569               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5570               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5571               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5572               	; 			'
5573               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5574               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5575               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5576               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5577               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5578               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5579               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31

6441               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN

     C5A4 21AE

     C5B0 C64E     
6447               cv282
6448               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6449               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6450               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6451               	; 		'
6452               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6453               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6454               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6455               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6456               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6457               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6458               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6459               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6460               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6461               	; 		'
6462               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6463               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6464               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6465               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6466               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6467               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6468 C5B2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6775               	;
6776               	; 	' --- running
6777               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6778               	; 	'
6779               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6780               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6781               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6782               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6783               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6784               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6785               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6786               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6787               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6788               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

6998               	; 		' THE FIRST STEP OFF THE MARK IS ALWAYS HEARD. sfw is a distance --
6999               	; 		' a step every fifteen pixels -- so a short tap of the stick moved
7000               	; 		' him a few pixels and made NO sound at all. That does not read as
7001               	; 		' a short step, it reads as the controls being ignored, and it is
7002               	; 		' worst exactly where a player taps rather than holds: lining up a
7003               	; 		' jump, or edging around a hazard.
7004               	; 		'
7005               	; 		' Priming the accumulator to the threshold makes the very next pass
7006               	; 		' fire. It does not cheat the RATE: the trigger subtracts fifteen
7007               	; 		' rather than zeroing, so the pixels actually travelled still carry
7008               	; 		' into the following step and a held run comes out at 6.9 a second
7009               	; 		' either way.
7010               	; 		IF kmvl = 0 THEN sfw = 15
7011 C9C8 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KMVL,r0
     C9CA 2205

     C9D0 C9DA

     C9D4 0F00

     C9D8 2208     
7017               cv366
7018               	; 		' AN ODOMETER, NOT A CLOCK -- pixels travelled, exactly like
7019               	; 		' Harry's hanim. It counted PASSES once, which made his legs cycle
7020               	; 		' slower on the escalator screens while he still ran at the same
7021               	; 		' speed; that was fixed by counting the frame DELTA instead, which
7022               	; 		' traded one mismatch for another -- his legs then kept wall-clock
7023               	; 		' time while his body still moved once per pass, so his stride
7024               	; 		' slipped against the ground whenever the loop was busy.
7025               	; 		'
7026               	; 		' Distance settles it. A pose per 8 px at any frame rate, and a
7027               	; 		' footstep is a stride rather than a tick.

7142               	;
7143               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
7144               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
7145               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
7146               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
7147               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
7148               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
7149               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
7150               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
7151               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
7152               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
7153               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
7154               	; 	'
7155               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
7156               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
7157               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
7158               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
7159               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7600               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------ getting on one
7601               	; 	' ONE ROUTINE FOR BOTH WAYS ON, because everything follows from esy0 --
7602               	; 	' the height above this floor of the step being boarded. The staircase is
7603               	; 	' a straight line, so a step 4 px higher is 8 px further along it:
7604               	; 	'
7605               	; 	'     west  klx = 99 - 2*esy0        east  klx = 140 + 2*esy0
7606               	; 	'
7607               	; 	' Walking on, the feet are still on the floor and have to climb onto that
7608               	; 	' step, so the ride starts at 0 and `eson` runs the catch-up. Jumping on,
7609               	; 	' he is already at the step's height, so it starts there with none.
7610               	; esc_ride:
7611               cvb_ESC_RIDE

7924               	;
7925               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7926               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7927               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7928               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7929               	; 	'
7930               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7931               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7932               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7933               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7934               	; 	'
7935               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7936               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7937               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7938               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7939               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7940               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7941               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7942               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7943               	; 	'
7944               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7945               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7946               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7947               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7948               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7949               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7950               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7951               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7952               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7953               	; 	'
7954               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7955               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7956               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7957               	; wipe_shelf:
7958               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7959               	; 	wsn = 8
7960 D02A 0200  20 	li r0,2048   ; 8*256
     D02C 0800

8031               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
8032               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
8033               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
8034               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
8035               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
8036               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
8037               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
8038               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
8039               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
8040               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
8041               	; 	' nothing.
8042               	; 	'
8043               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
8044               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
8045               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
8046               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
8047               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to

8103               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
8104               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
8105               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
8106               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
8107               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
8108               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
8109               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
8110               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
8111               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
8112               	; 	'

8597               	; upd_obst:
8598               cvb_UPD_OBST
8599               	; 	' ONE ACCUMULATOR PER KIND, NOT PER SLOT. Every hazard of a kind moves at
8600               	; 	' that kind's speed, so three calls a pass serve all eight slots.
8601               	; 	'
8602               	; 	' Per-slot would have meant up to eight calls of O(fdv) work every pass --
8603               	; 	' the positive feedback loop CLAUDE.md 3A warns about, where a slow pass is
8604               	; 	' made slower by the very code meant to take the loop rate out of the
8605               	; 	' game's behaviour. It also saves the eight bytes an obacc() would cost,
8606               	; 	' and ColecoVision RAM is the tighter budget.
8607               	; 	pacc = oacb
8608 D532 D820  54 	movb @cvb_OACB,@cvb_PACC
     D534 20C7     
     D536 211E

     D588 2142     
     D58A 20F3     
8640               	; 	' THE BOUNCE PHASE, ON THE SAME CLOCK AS THE JUMP AND AT ITS OLD PERIOD.
8641               	; 	'
8642               	; 	' The jump has always advanced by the frame delta (`kjf = kjf + fdv`) while
8643               	; 	' the bounce advanced once per PASS -- so whether a jump cleared a ball
8644               	; 	' depended on how busy the screen was, and the frame-by-frame property
8645               	; 	' checkball.py proves was exact only at fdv = 1.
8646               	; 	'
8647               	; 	' Stepping the phase by fdv directly would fix the clock and wreck the
8648               	; 	' game: a 32-phase arc would drop from about 1.35 s to 0.55 s, balls
8649               	; 	' bouncing 2.4x faster everywhere. So it gets a fraction of its own --
8650               	; 	' 25/64 of a phase per frame is 23.4 phases a second, against the 23.8 it
8651               	; 	' runs at today. Same bounce, now the same clock as the thing that has to
8652               	; 	' clear it.
8653               	; 	pacc = obac
8654 D58C D820  54 	movb @cvb_OBAC,@cvb_PACC
     D58E 20E6

8870               	;
8871               	; 	' ONE STEP OF A FRACTIONAL SPEED, FOR WHOEVER IS MOVING.
8872               	; 	'
8873               	; 	' `pacc` in and out, `psp64` in (sixty-fourths of a pixel per FRAME),
8874               	; 	' `pspd` out (whole pixels this pass). CVBasic has no locals, so the caller
8875               	; 	' stages its own accumulator in and takes it back out; that costs four
8876               	; 	' statements against the fourteen this used to be, written out per actor.
8877               	; 	'
8878               	; 	' Harry had the only copy. Kelly, the hazards and the bounce phase all need
8879               	; 	' the same thing -- everything the round clock judges has to advance by
8880               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAMES, or the loop rate becomes a difficulty dial and where the
8881               	; 	' player stands decides the outcome (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
8882               	; 	'
8883               	; 	' TWO DRAINS CAP THE STEP AT 2 PER FRAME whatever psp64 says (CLAUDE.md
8884               	; 	' 3A: N drains cap the speed at N, and the surplus leaks into a byte that
8885               	; 	' wraps). Every caller is under that: Kelly 102/64 = 1.6, the fastest
8886               	; 	' hazard 76/64 = 1.2, Harry 57/64 = 0.9, the bounce phase 25/64 = 0.4.
8887               	; 	'
8888               	; 	' FIVE FRAMES, AND THREE WAS TOO FEW. At 2 px a frame five frames is 10 px,
8889               	; 	' and to skip a position window an actor must start outside it on one side
8890               	; 	' and land outside on the other, which needs 12. Three was the original bug
8891               	; 	' in miniature: the lift screen runs at almost exactly 20 passes a second,
8892               	; 	' which IS fdv = 3, so every hitch there lost a frame. The clamp exists for
8893               	; 	' a genuine stall and at five it only fires for one.
8894               	; pace_step:
8895               cvb_PACE_STEP
8896               	; 	pfd = fdv

9385               cv593
9386               	;
9387               	; 	' THE SIXTEENTH-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen
9388               	; 	' (2), so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here
9389               	; 	' and is spent as whole pixels. THREE IFs, not a loop and not a divide:
9390               	; 	' `%` and `/` both compile to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this
9391               	; 	' runs every pass.
9392               	; 	'
9393               	; 	' THE NUMBER OF DRAINS IS A CEILING ON THE SPEED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
9394               	; 	' POINT OF THIS COMMENT. Each IF can spend one whole pixel, so N drains
9395               	; 	' cap hspd at N px per pass NO MATTER WHAT hsp64 SAYS. The invariant is:
9396               	; 	'
9397               	; 	'     hacc is under 16 on entry, and hsp64 is at most 48,
9398               	; 	'     so hacc is under 64 here and three drains always suffice.
9399               	; 	'
9400               	; 	' KEEP hsp64 <= 48. This was quarter-pixels with TWO drains and the same
9401               	; 	' invariant written the same way -- "hsp4 is at most 8, so it can never
9402               	; 	' need a third" -- and it was correct until somebody set hsp4 = 9 without
9403               	; 	' re-reading it. The crook then ran at a flat 2.0 px/pass while every
9404               	; 	' comment, the design table and assets/checkchase.py all said 2.25, and
9405               	; 	' the surplus quarter leaked into hacc and wrapped the byte every 256
9406               	; 	' passes. Nothing failed; he simply arrived two thirds of a screen short
9407               	; 	' of his own escape. checkchase.py now counts these IFs and refuses a
9408               	; 	' speed the drains cannot deliver.
9409               	; 	'
9410               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
9411               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
9412               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
9413               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
9414               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
9415               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
9416               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
9417               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
9418               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
9419               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
9420               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
9421               	; 	' flight looking busy.
9422               	; 	'
9423               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
9424               	; 	'
9425               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
9426               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
9427               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
9428               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
9429               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
9430               	; 	'
9431               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
9432               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
9433               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
9434               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
9435               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN
9436 DB40 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HRUN,r0
     DB42 21C8     
9437 DB44 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     DB46 0100

9517               	;
9518               	; 	' HE WALKS BY REAL TIME, NOT BY LOOP PASS -- one accumulate-and-drain per
9519               	; 	' ELAPSED FRAME rather than per pass. Everything that moves here used to be
9520               	; 	' per pass while the CLOCK is paced by the frame delta, so a busy screen
9521               	; 	' slowed Harry in real seconds and the clock did not slow with him: the
9522               	; 	' same uninterrupted escape finished with nine timer units in hand from a
9523               	; 	' light screen and none at all from the lift screen, decided by where the
9524               	; 	' player happened to be standing. Now he covers the same ground per second
9525               	; 	' wherever anybody is (DESIGN.md 0f-ter).
9526               	; 	'
9527               	; 	' A LOOP, NOT A MULTIPLY. `*` compiles to a real TMS9900 MPY, and reading a
9528               	; 	' 16-bit variable straight after one returns the product's HIGH word
9529               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A). fdv is 1..3 here, so this is at most three adds.
9530               	; 	'
9531               	; 	' SIXTY-FOURTHS, AND ONLY TWO DRAINS -- both fall out of accumulating per
9532               	; 	' FRAME instead of per pass. He covers under a pixel a frame, so two drains
9533               	; 	' are ample where the per-pass version needed three; and the finer unit is
9534               	; 	' what makes the speed tunable at all. In sixteenths one step was about six
9535               	; 	' SECONDS of his route, so nothing landed near the buzzer; in sixty-fourths
9536               	; 	' it is about one and a half. Keep hsp64 <= 128: hacc is under 64 on entry,
9537               	; 	' so 64 + 128 stays inside a byte and two drains always suffice.
9538               	; 	'
9539               	; 	' AND THE STEP IS CLAMPED TO THREE FRAMES' WORTH, WHICH IS NOT A DETAIL.
9540               	; 	' Two tests sample his position once a pass and neither interpolates:
9541               	; 	'
9542               	; 	'   ARRIVAL   `hdx < 6` around the escalator or the roof door -- an ELEVEN
9543               	; 	'             pixel window. A step over 10 px can jump clean across it and
9544               	; 	'             he never arrives at all.
9545               	; 	'   THE CATCH `hdd < CATCHR` is 12, a 23-wide band, and Kelly closes 4 of
9546               	; 	'             it himself -- so Harry over ~18 px could pass through the Kop
9547               	; 	'             between two passes without either sample being inside it.
9548               	; 	'
9549               	; 	' The clamp and the drains live in pace_step now, with the reasoning; the
9550               	; 	' numbers above are what sized them. Measured: TIME 02 on the lift screen
9551               	; 	' against TIME 04 on a light one when the clamp was 3, and 00 against 09
9552               	; 	' before any of this.
9553               	; 	'
9554               	; 	' The ESCALATOR RIDE is deliberately untouched: it returns above this,
9555               	; 	' clocked one step per pass to stay locked to the moving staircase. A rider
9556               	; 	' advanced by the frame delta either drifts off the treads or makes the
9557               	; 	' steps run backwards (CLAUDE.md 3A, DESIGN.md 0f). It is about 3% of his
9558               	; 	' journey.
9559               	; 	'
9560               	; 	' `hanim` still advances by hspd and is still an ODOMETER -- pixels
9561               	; 	' travelled, not time -- so the run cycle stays locked to the ground at any
9562               	; 	' rate. A 9 px step moves it exactly one pose of its 8 px beat.
9563               	; 	pacc = hacc
9564 DBDC D820  54 	movb @cvb_HACC,@cvb_PACC
     DBDE 21A9     
     DBE0 211E     
9565               	; 	psp64 = hsp64

     DBE4 20D3     
     DBE6 2126

9726               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
9727               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
9728               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
9729               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
9730               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
9731               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
9732               	; 	'
9733               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
9734               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any
9735               	; 	' speed, including the frames where the accumulator gives him nothing.

10000               	;
10001               	; 	' ======================================================================
10002               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
10003               	; 	' ======================================================================
10004               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
10005               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
10006               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
10007               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
10008               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
10009               	; 	'
10010               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
10011               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
10012               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
10013               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
10014               	; 	' broken.
10015               	; 	'
10016               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
10017               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
10018               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
10019               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
10020               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
10021               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
10022               	; 	'
10023               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
10024               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
10025               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
10026               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
10027               	; coll_obst:
10028               cvb_COLL_OBST
10029               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
10030 DF4C D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DF4E 21AE

     DF52 0300

10326               	; 	' ONE PASS LONGER THAN THE SOUND, because the sound does not start here.
10327               	; 	' sfh is a latch that sfx_tick consumes on the NEXT pass, so the note
10328               	; 	' runs passes 2..HITSND+1 of the freeze and this ends with it.
10329               	; 	hfz = HITSND + 1
10330 E1CC 0200  20 	li r0,5376   ; 21*256
     E1CE 1500     
10331 E1D0 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_HFZ
     E1D2 218A     
10332               	; 	' THE FLOOR CLEARS WHEN YOU ARE HIT, and stays clear until you re-enter
10333               	; 	' the screen. Nine seconds is a heavy penalty on a fifty-unit clock, and
10334               	; 	' taking it while still standing among the things that charged it -- with
10335               	; 	' a second hazard 48 px behind the first -- is how one mistake becomes
10336               	; 	' three. Clearing the row makes the penalty a single event you can walk
10337               	; 	' away from.
10338               	; 	'
10339               	; 	' BOTH SLOTS, WHATEVER IS IN THEM. Radios used to be exempt: they are
10340               	; 	' CHARACTERS stamped into the name table rather than sprites, so zeroing
10341               	; 	' the kind would have stopped them colliding while leaving them plainly
10342               	; 	' visible on the shelf, and a fixture that is still there has to still be
10343               	; 	' there. That was a reason to UNDRAW them, not a reason to keep them --
10344               	; 	' and it left the one hazard that cannot leave on its own as the only one
10345               	; 	' the mercy did not cover. haz_off now takes the characters with it.
10346               	; 	'
10347               	; 	' NOTHING RESTORES IT HERE. load_band repopulates the band from the
10348               	; 	' template, and it runs only on a seam crossing or a round start, so
10349               	; 	' "until the screen is re-entered" costs no state and no timer: leave and
10350               	; 	' come back and the hazards are simply placed again.
10351               	; 	'
10352               	; 	' THE CALL ITSELF IS IN THE MAIN LOOP, at the end of the freeze -- see
10353               	; 	' the hfz block there for why.
10354               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,
10355               	; 	' which dropped him straight down out of mid-air onto whatever he happened
10356               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
10357               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
10358               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
10359               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
10360               	; 	'
10361               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
10362               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.
10363               	; 	IF klst <> ST_JUMP THEN
10364 E1D4 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     E1D6 21AE

     E1DA 0100

10521               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
10522               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
10523               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
10524               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
10525               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
10526               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
10527               	; 	'
10528               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
10529               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
10530               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
10531               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.
10532               	; 	IF klx > hx THEN hdd = klx - hx ELSE hdd = hx - klx

10564               	; draw_actors:
10565               cvb_DRAW_ACTORS
10566               	; 	' RIDING WITH THE DOORS SHUT: HIDE HIM AND DO NOT DRAW HIM AT ALL.
10567               	; 	'
10568               	; 	' This test used to sit AFTER his sprites had been written, so every pass
10569               	; 	' placed him at his old floor and then hid him again. That is not free.
10570               	; 	' CVBasic's SPRITE writes a RAM mirror which the vblank ISR copies to
10571               	; 	' VRAM, so a vblank landing BETWEEN the draw and the hide latches the
10572               	; 	' visible state for a frame -- and draw_actors is long (Kelly, Harry,
10573               	; 	' eight obstacles) while a pass on the lift screen spans two or three
10574               	; 	' frames, so it happened constantly. He flickered at the floor he had
10575               	; 	' just left, which reads as a drawing bug rather than as a journey.
10576               	; 	'
10577               	; 	' Deciding BEFORE drawing costs one test and cannot race the ISR at all:
10578               	; 	' the mirror never holds a position to be caught with.
10579               	; 	IF klst = ST_ELEV THEN
10580 E36E D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0

10657               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
10658               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
10659               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
10660               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
10661               	; 	'
10662               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
10663               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
10664               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
10665               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
10666               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
10667               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
10668               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

10813               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
10814               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
10815               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
10816               	; 		'
10817               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
10818               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
10819               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
10820               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
10821               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
10822               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
10823 E550 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E552 3800     
10824 E554 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_KQ
     E556 21D9     
10825               	; 		' BITS 3 AND 4, NOT 2 AND 3 -- A POSE EVERY 8 PIXELS, which is
10826               	; 		' Harry's beat (hanim AND 8 / AND 16) and now means the same thing,
10827               	; 		' because both counters hold pixels travelled. On the old 4-count
10828               	; 		' beat an odometer would have flipped his legs every single pass at
10829               	; 		' 4 px a pass, which is a blur rather than a run. checkanim reads
10830               	; 		' these bits out of the source, and fails if two bands of one figure
10831               	; 		' run on different ones -- so the body below moves with them.
10832               	; 		IF kanim AND 8 THEN kq = kq + 4

11060               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
11061               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
11062               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
11063               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
11064               	; 	'
11065               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
11066               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
11067               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
11068               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
11069               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
11070               	; 	'
11071               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
11072               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
11073               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
11074               	; 	' is what running looks like.
11075               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
11076               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
11077               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.

11179               cv782
11180               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
11181               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
11182               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
11183               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
11184               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
11185               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
11186               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
11187               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
11188               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
11189               	; 	'
11190               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
11191               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
11192               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
11193               	; 	' a jump.
11194               	; 	'
11195               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
11196               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
11197               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
11198               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
11199               	; 	'
11200               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
11201               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
11202               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
11203               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
11204               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
11205               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
11206               	; 	'
11207               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
11208               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
11209               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
11210               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
11211               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
11212               	; 	'
11213               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
11214               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
11215               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
11216               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
11217               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

11695 EC0C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11696 EC0E 0450  20 	b *r0
11697               	;
11698               	; 	' ======================================================================
11699               	; 	' THE SCANNER
11700               	; 	' ======================================================================
11701               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
11702               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
11703               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
11704               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
11705               	; 	'
11706               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
11707               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
11708               	; 	'
11709               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
11710               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
11711               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
11712               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
11713               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
11714               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
11715               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
11716               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
11717               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
11718               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
11719               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
11720               	; esc_tick:
11721               cvb_ESC_TICK
11722               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
11723               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
11724               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
11725               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11726               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
11727 EC10 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EC12 219A     
11728 EC14 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     EC16 0100     
11729 EC18 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11730 EC1A 1402  14 	jhe cv823
11731 EC1C 0460  28 	b @cv822
     EC1E EC34     
11732               cv823
11733               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
11734 EC20 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EC22 219A     
11735 EC24 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     EC26 0700     
11736 EC28 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11737 EC2A 1A02  14 	jl cv825
11738 EC2C 0460  28 	b @cv824
     EC2E EC34     
11739               cv825
11740 EC30 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11741 EC32 0450  20 	b *r0
11742               cv824
11743               	; 	END IF
11744               cv822
11745               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
11746               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
11747               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
11748               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
11749               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
11750               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
11751               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
11752               	; 	'
11753               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
11754               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
11755               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
11756               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
11757               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
11758               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
11759               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
11760               	; 	escp = escp + 1
11761 EC34 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EC36 20B1     
11762 EC38 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     EC3A 0100     
11763 EC3C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EC3E 20B1     
11764               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
11765 EC40 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     EC42 0400     
11766 EC44 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
11767 EC46 1402  14 	jhe cv827
11768 EC48 0460  28 	b @cv826
     EC4A EC52     
11769               cv827
11770 EC4C 04C0  14 	clr r0
11771 EC4E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     EC50 20B1     
11772               cv826
11773               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
11774               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
11775               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
11776               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
11777               	; 	' 7 an east one.
11778               	; 	'
11779               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
11780               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
11781               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
11782               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
11783               	; 	'
11784               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
11785               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
11786               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
11787               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
11788               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
11789               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
11790               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
11791               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
11792               	; 	'
11793               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
11794               	; 	' move at all.
11795               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN
11796 EC52 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     EC54 219A

     EC5A ECE2

11916               	; 	' stops the radar touching the shop floor above and the screen edge
