12056               	;
12057               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
12058               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
12059               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
12060               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
12061               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
12062               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
12063               	; 	'
12064               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
12065               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
12066               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
12067               	; 	'
12068               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
12069               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
12070               	; 	'   +2  GREY
12071               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
12072               	; 	'
12073               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
12074               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
12075               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
12076               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
12077               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
12078               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
12079               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
12080               	; 	'
12081               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
12082               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
12083               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
12084               	; 	'
12085               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
12086               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
12087               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
12088               	; scan_furn:
12089               cvb_SCAN_FURN

12136               	; 		' on screen could reflect.

12283               	;
12284               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
12285               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry
12286               	; 	' their own colour (scan_mark).
12287               	; 	'
12288               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
12289               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
12290               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
12291               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
12292               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
12293               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.
12294               	; scan_escc:
12295               cvb_SCAN_ESCC
12296               	; 	#sdx = #sda + 8192
12297 F002 C020  34 	mov @cvb__SDA,r0
     F004 20A2     
12298 F006 0220  22 	ai r0,8192
     F008 2000

12429               	;
12430               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
12431               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
12432               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
12433               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
12434               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
12435               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12436               	; scan_wipe:
12437               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
12438               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
12439               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
12440               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
12441               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
12442               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
12443               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
12444               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
12445               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
12446               	; 	'
12447               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
12448               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
12449               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
12450               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
12451               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
12452               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
12453               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
12454               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
12455               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
12456               	; 	'
12457               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
12458               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
12459               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
12460               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
12461               	; 	'
12462               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
12463               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
12464               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
12465 F128 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F12A 1680

12532               	;
12533               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
12534               	; 	'
12535               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
12536               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
12537               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
12538               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
12539               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
12540               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
12541               	; 	'
12542               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
12543               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
12544               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
12545               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
12546               	; 	'
12547               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
12548               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
12549               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
12550               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
12551               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
12552               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
12553               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
12554               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
12555               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
12556               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
12557               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

12641 F270 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

12643               	;
12644               	; 	' sdsc/sdxp/sdlv in, sdx/sdy out. The bias and the row meanings that used
12645               	; 	' to be explained twice are explained here once.
12646               	; 	'
12647               	; 	' 167, NOT 168. The VDP puts a sprite's top line at y + 1, so a sprite
12648               	; 	' asked for the canvas's own row lands one pixel low -- which on a

12650               	; 	' yellow floor line. The character markers this replaced were poked

13373 F7D6 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     F7D8 AA60     
13374               	;
13375               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
13376               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
13377               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
13378               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
13379               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
13380               	; 	'
13381               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
13382               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
13383               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
13384               	; 	'
13385               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
13386               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
13387               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
13388               	; 	'
13389               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable

13444               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS TALLY B -- a blip that COUNTS rather than a buzz that
13445               	; 	' ratchets. Two frames on and two off at 1,036 Hz (divisor 108), against
13446               	; 	' the 373 Hz it was: the pitch is what separates a count from the
13447               	; 	' timer's own low tick, and the shorter gap makes ten of them a run
13448               	; 	' rather than a queue.
13449               	; 	'
13450               	; 	' Channel 2 stays. The effect table uses it for the upper voice of a

     F8C6 F818     
13507               	;
13508               	; 	' AND IT ENDS ON ITS LAST BLIP -- there is no closing accent.
13509               	; 	'
13510               	; 	' Two were tried. The bench's own ending for this variant rose a fourth,
13511               	; 	' to 2,542 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek -- the same fault the
13512               	; 	' pickup was rewritten for. Dropping it an OCTAVE BELOW the ticks, to
13513               	; 	' 518 Hz, was reported the same way, and that one cannot be too high by
13514               	; 	' any measure: it is the lowest note in the effect.
13515               	; 	'
13516               	; 	' So the complaint is not the pitch, it is the extra note. A tally is
13517               	; 	' arithmetic, and arithmetic finishes when the last term is added; a
13518               	; 	' flourish after it is a second event the player has to interpret, and
13519               	; 	' the HUD has already said the clock is empty. The count stops when the
13520               	; 	' counting stops.
13521               	;
13522               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
13523               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
13524               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
13525               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
13526               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
13527               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
13528               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
13529               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
13530               	; 	'
13531               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
13532               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
13533               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13534               	;
13535               	; do_escape:
13536               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
13537               	; 	escapd = 0
13538 F8C8 04C0  14 	clr r0
13539 F8CA D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCAPD
     F8CC 2112

13582               	; 	'

13584               	; 	' the store being deleted and then two words on an empty field -- which
13585               	; 	' reads as the program ending rather than as the game ending. The store

13589               	; 	' HUD_BG (dark blue), so a row of SPACES is a solid dark blue bar. A blank
13590               	; 	' row above and below the text is a frame for the price of two strings and

13596               	; 	' to exactly thirteen so the box has straight edges, and column 10 puts it

13747               	; 		' globals, so a starting Krook or Kop count typed on the setup
13748               	; 		' screen otherwise applied to every game after it -- including one
13749               	; 		' started by somebody who never saw the screen and had no way to
13750               	; 		' know why they began on level 12 with two Kops. Zeroing them lets
13751               	; 		' new_game's own defaults take over; typing 8-3-8 again sets them

13767 FAA6 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     FAA8 AA60     
13768               	;
13769               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
13770               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
13771               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
13772               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
13773               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
13774               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
13775               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
13776               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
13777               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
13778               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
13779               	; 	' Harry was caught.
13780               	; snd_off:
13781               cvb_SND_OFF
13782               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL sfx_tick CAN WRITE, AND CHANNEL 3 IS ONE OF THEM. This
13783               	; 	' routine was written when the footstep was a pair of tones on channel 0
13784               	; 	' and it still named only 0, 1 and 2 after the footstep moved to the
13785               	; 	' NOISE channel. So a step ringing when Harry was caught was never turned
13786               	; 	' off: it hissed through the whole bonus tally and did not stop until the
13787               	; 	' next round's first footstep happened to reset it.
13788               	; 	'
13789               	; 	' Zeroing sot does NOT silence it. The counter only emits its note-off on
13790               	; 	' the pass it reaches zero, so setting it to zero by hand skips the very
13791               	; 	' write that would have stopped the sound.
13792               	; 	SOUND 0,0,0
13793 FAAA 04C0  14 	clr r0
13794 FAAC 0202  20 	li r2,>8000
     FAAE 8000

     FAB2 A980

13908               	; 	' ======================================================================
13909               	; 	' SOUND
13910               	; 	' ======================================================================
13911               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
13912               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
13913               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
13914               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
13915               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

13960               	; 	' A PRIZE IS AN ARPEGGIO -- testsounds PICKUP C. C5, E5, G5, then a fade
13961               	; 	' on the top note: it RISES, which is what makes it read as a reward
13962               	; 	' rather than as an event, and it ARRIVES, because a major triad resolves
13963               	; 	' where an arbitrary rise just stops.
13964               	; 	'
13965               	; 	' It was two notes struck together on two channels and held. That needed
13966               	; 	' both channels for one sound, said nothing in particular, and had no
13967               	; 	' shape at all -- a chord is a texture, and this wants to be a phrase.
13968               	; 	'
13969               	; 	' NOT THE MEASURED ONE, ON PURPOSE. The 2600 tops its pickup out at
13970               	; 	' 2543 Hz, and a square wave there is a shriek; TIA's softer timbre
13971               	; 	' carries it and the SN76489's does not. This peaks at 784 Hz. Copying the
13972               	; 	' recording would have been faithful and unpleasant, and the player earns
13973               	; 	' this sound dozens of times a round.
13974               	; 	IF sfp = 1 THEN

14144               	;
14145               	; 	' the jump sweep: divisor falling = pitch rising
14146               	; 	' THE JUMP IS A WARBLE, NOT A SWEEP -- testsounds variant A, measured off
14147               	; 	' the 2600 at about 415 Hz alternating with 188 for roughly 280 ms.
14148               	; 	'
14149               	; 	' It used to be a rising sweep, 500 down to 120 in steps of 40. That was
14150               	; 	' invented rather than measured, and it had the wrong SHAPE: a sweep reads
14151               	; 	' as something departing, which is a fine idea and is not what the original
14152               	; 	' does. Eight ticks alternating is four cycles of warble, which is what the
14153               	; 	' recording shows.
14154               	; 	'
14155               	; 	' 270 is 415 Hz and 595 is 188 Hz (divisor = 3579545 / (32 * f), so a
14156               	; 	' SMALLER divisor is a HIGHER note -- the pair reads backwards from how it
14157               	; 	' is written).
14158               	; 	IF swt > 0 THEN

14214               cv1009
14215               	;
14216               	; 	' THE LOW-TIME WARNING IS SILENT. There was a beep a second under ten, and
14217               	; 	' it was the wrong instrument for the job: the last ten seconds are the
14218               	; 	' busiest part of a round, so a repeating tone arrives exactly when the
14219               	; 	' player most needs to hear the hazards, and it fought the prize arpeggio
14220               	; 	' for channel 2 as well. The flash says the same thing and says it in the
14221               	; 	' place the player is already looking.
14222               	;
14223               	; 	' FOOTSTEPS ARE NOISE, AND ON THE NOISE CHANNEL -- testsounds variant A.
14224               	; 	'
14225               	; 	' They were two alternating TONES on channel 0, which is neither what the
14226               	; 	' original does nor a good use of a channel: the recording says a footstep
14227               	; 	' is a short burst of white noise, and channel 3 was sitting unused while
14228               	; 	' the footsteps and the jump sweep shared channel 0 and had to take turns.
14229               	; 	'
14230               	; 	' TESTSOUNDS RUN D: a SHORT tick at the measured RATE.
14231               	; 	'
14232               	; 	' The two dials are independent and both were tried at the bench. The
14233               	; 	' 2600 plays a 64 ms burst every 165 ms -- six a second -- and variant A
14234               	; 	' copies both. Ten a second was tried on the way here and reads as
14235               	; 	' hurrying rather than running. What D changes is not the cadence but the
14236               	; 	' TICK: two frames rather than four, and a notch louder to carry at half
14237               	; 	' the length. Same six a second, less of it.
14238               	; 	'
14239               	; 	' EIGHT A SECOND, AND THE ACCUMULATOR NOW CARRIES ITS REMAINDER.
14240               	; 	'
14241               	; 	' sfw counted pixels and the trigger did `sfw = 0`, THROWING AWAY whatever
14242               	; 	' was past the threshold. kspd is about four pixels a pass, so a threshold
14243               	; 	' of 17 did not fire at 17 -- it fired at the first multiple past it, about
14244               	; 	' 20, and every rate this was ever set to came out slower than the number
14245               	; 	' said: the "six a second" was 5.2 and the "ten" that read as hurrying was
14246               	; 	' really 8.7. Subtracting the threshold instead keeps the fraction, so the
14247               	; 	' rate is what it claims to be at any walking speed.
14248               	; 	'
14249               	; 	' 104 px/s over 15 px is 6.9 a second -- between the 8.0 that read as
14250               	; 	' slightly hurried and the 6.1 that read as trudging. The dial is this one
14251               	; 	' constant and the subtraction below, which must stay threshold + 1 or the
14252               	; 	' carry is wrong.
14253               	; 	'
14254               	; 	' SOT = 2 IS THE SHORTEST TICK THERE IS, and 1 is SILENCE. The decay block
14255               	; 	' below runs LATER IN THE SAME sfx_tick, so a count of 1 is decremented to
14256               	; 	' zero on the pass that set it and the note-off fires before the sound has
14257               	; 	' lasted any time at all. 2 survives to the next pass, which is one pass of
14258               	; 	' sound -- about 42 ms against the bench's 33, as close as a per-pass timer
14259               	; 	' gets, and the reason the bench and the game cannot sound identical.
14260               	; 	'
14261               	; 	' Register 4 is white noise at the fastest rate.
14262               	; 	'
14263               	; 	' VOLUME 9, WHICH IS UNDER EVERYTHING ELSE ON PURPOSE. The hit, the prize
14264               	; 	' and the jump are 12 and 13, and they happen ONCE; footsteps play for the
14265               	; 	' whole round. At 13 they were level with the events they are supposed to
14266               	; 	' sit beneath, so the effects that carry information had nothing to stand
14267               	; 	' out from. The tone version was 7 for the same reason -- noise does read
14268               	; 	' quieter than a tone at the same number, which is why this is 9 and not
14269               	; 	' back to 7.
14270               	; 	IF sfw > 14 THEN
14271 FDF2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_SFW,r0
     FDF4 2208     
14272 FDF6 0201  20 	li r1,3840   ; 15*256
     FDF8 0F00

     FE00 FE38

14392 FEF0 0450  20 	b *r0
14393               	;
14394               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
14395               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
14396               	; 	'
14397               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
14398               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
14399               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
14400               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
14401               	; 	'
14402               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
14403               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
14404               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
14405               	; 	'
14406               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
14407               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
14408               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
14409               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
14410               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
14411               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
14412               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
14413               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
14414               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
14415               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
14416               	; 	#if TI994A
14417               	; 	BANK 1
14418      010C     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

14425               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
14426               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14427               	; 	'
14428               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
14429               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
14430               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
14431               	; 	'
14432               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
14433               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
14434               	; 	' letter after it.
14435               	;
14436               	; font_bits:
14437               cvb_FONT_BITS

14556               	;
14557               	; 	' THE COLOUR TABLE, 472 bytes of the same value ($14 = black ink on dark blue).
14558               	; 	'
14559               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE COLOR 32,59,font_col, which is ONE synchronous call that
14560               	; 	' writes all three screen thirds (define_color always does the LDIRVM3 triple
14561               	; 	' copy). It replaced a VPOKE loop that wrote this one value 1,416 times paced by
14562               	; 	' 24 WAITs -- the largest scripted delay in the boot, and the reason the title
14563               	; 	' screen used to fill in visibly rather than appearing at once.
14564               	; 	'
14565               	; 	' EIGHT BYTES PER CHARACTER, not one. Supply fewer and DEFINE COLOR reads
14566               	; 	' whatever follows in ROM as colour data, with no error at build or run time.
14567               	;
14568               	; font_col:
14569               cvb_FONT_COL
14570               	; 	DATA BYTE $14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14,$14	' space

14688               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
14689               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
14690               	; 	'
14691               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
14692               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
14693               	; 	' being small sprites.
14694               	; 	'
14695               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
14696               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
14697               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
14698               	; 	'
14699               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
14700               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
14701               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
14702               	;
14703               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
14704               cvb_SPR_KELLY
14705               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

15807               	;
15808               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
15809               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
15810               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
15811               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
15812               	;
15813               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
15814               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
15815               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
15816               	; 	' unreadable.
15817               	;
15818               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
15819               cvb_SCAN_COL3
15820               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
15821 740A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     740C EEEE     
     740E ECEC     
     7410 ECBC

     7418 ECBC

15930               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
15931               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
15932               	; 	'
15933               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
15934               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
15935               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
15936               	; 	'
15937               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
15938               	; 	' blit is
15939               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
15940               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
15941               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
15942               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
15943               	; 	'
15944               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
15945               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
15946               	; 	'
15947               	; 	' template ids:
15948               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
15949               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
15950               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
15951               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
15952               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
15953               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
15954               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
15955               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
15956               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
15957               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
15958               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
15959               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
15960               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
15961               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
15962               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
15963               	;
15964               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
15965               cvb_STOR_TPL
